PROCEEDINGS OF THE

§21100. PROPERTY OF DISSOLVED LODGE.
In the event of the dissolution of a Lodge, the Grand Secretary, or his
duly authorized representative, shall, in such manner as shall seem to
him most judicious:
A.

Proceed at once to receive its funds on hand, except the Life
Membership Fund;

B.

Collect outstanding dues;

C.

Dispose of its jewels, furniture, and other property; and

D.

Place the proceeds thereof, after the payment of all just debts
and necessary expenses, among the funds of Grand Lodge.

In the event of the dissolution of a Lodge the assets of its Life
Membership Fund shall be delivered to the Grand Treasurer, who shall
hold it as a trust fund pending its disposition. The Grand Treasurer
shall compute the interest of each living life member in the Fund and
the interest of Grand Lodge therein, on a just and equitable basis.
When the interests are determined by Grand Lodge, the Grand
Treasurer shall distribute the Fund, or the proceeds of a sale thereof, in
accordance with that determination.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Kirkpatrick’s Recommendation No. 04-03 presents an
extensive revision of Article VIII of Part II of the California Masonic Code
regarding the use and preservation of constituent Lodge funds and investments.
The state rationale of the proposal is to update the California Masonic Code to
reflect modern investment practices and to provide safeguards for Lodge assets.
While the Recommendation would change the language in many code sections,
there are four changes in particular that are of special significance. They are as
follows:
1.

Section 21030 would be amended to require every Lodge to administer
its assets through Trustees elected by the Lodge members. The
Trustees would be permitted to invest Lodge assets only in accordance
with a written investment policy which the Lodge has approved.

2.

Section 21030 would be further amended to require the Trustees to
retain the services of a Registered Investment Advisor; however, the
Trustees would not be required to follow the advice given. The
Trustees would also be permitted to allow the Advisor to directly
manage the Lodge assets.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

167

3.

Section 21030 would be further amended so as to require every Lodge
to maintain in readily available funds an amount equal to six months of
the Lodge’s annual budgeted expenses. In other words, every Lodge
would be required to have on had at all times a six month operating
reserve.

4.

Section 21090 would be amended to change the method of calculating
how much of a Lodge’s assets could be expended for social and
fraternal purposes. Currently, the California Masonic Code permits a
Lodge to use up to 30% of its annual investment income for
refreshment, community involvement, charitable activity and other
programs that will benefit the local community. This Recommendation
would change the 30% of income ule to an amount equal to four percent
of the fair market value of the Lodge’s investments that could be
expended for these purposes. That is, the amount that a Lodge could
expend in any given year would be limited to a percentage of the fair
market value of the Lodge’s current assets and not to a percentage of
the Lodge’s current income.

The Constitution of the California Grand Lodge provides authority for
Grand Lodge to govern its constituent Lodges, and Grand Lodge has always
exercised some level of supervision over its constituent Lodges’ investments.
Over the years, that level of supervision has increased from time to time, and
Grand Lodge restrictions and limitations on constituent Lodge investments have
increased, always for the purpose of ensuring the continued safety and security
of constituent Lodge assets, and this the stability of Grand Lodge itself.
This Recommendation is consistent with the Policy and General
Purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Investments:
Most Worshipful, Brethren of Garnd Lodge, Grand Master
Kirkpatrick’s Recommendation No. 3 entitled “Lodge Funds and Investments”
requires a 5/6 majority to pass on the first instance. The language of this
legislation is adequate to achieve its several stated objectives.
First, it modifies the California Masonic Code to incorporate language
and terminology currently employed by the investment community.
Second, it clarifies, simplifies and facilitates the investment process for
lodges and Masonic Hall or Center Associations.
And finally, it assists the leaders of our lodges in managing and
investing their funds prudently to promote proper financial stewardship.
Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Grand Master Kirkpatrick’s Recommendation No. 2004-04
Executive Committee Appointments to Constitutional Boards
At present the California Masonic Code makes members of the Grand
Lodge Executive Committee ex-officio trustees of the several constitutional
boards. The Deputy Grand Master and Grand Wardens serve as ex-officio
trustees of the Masonic Homes of California (MHC). The Deputy Grand Master
also serves as an ex-officio trustee of the California Masonic Foundation (CMF),
and the Junior Grand Warden serves as an ex-officio trustee of the California
Masonic Memorial Temple (CMMT).
Executive Committee representation on these various constitutional
boards is important. From time to time, however, the needs of the various
boards and the professional and fraternal obligations of the Executive Committee
members make the current law over-burdensome and inefficient. For example,
the Junior Grand Warden serves on the CMMT and the Deputy Grand Master on
the CMF. These days, those boards meet on the same day and in the same
location. It would make more sense and be more economical to appoint only one
Executive Committee member to serve on both boards.
Because the organizational structure and needs of the various boards
cannot be predicted, flexibility with these appointments would be advantageous
to both the Grand Lodge and to the constitutional boards.
I, therefore, recommend that the Grand Master be given the
responsibility to appoint members of the Executive Committee to the
constitutional boards on an annual basis provided that at least two members of
the Executive Committee are appointed to the MHC and at least one to each of
the CMF and the CMMT.
If you adopt this recommendation, the following changes will be made
to the Code.
Section 8020 of the California Masonic Code will be amended to read
as follows:
§8020. COMPOSITION OF BOARD.
The Trustees of the Masonic Homes of California shall be 12 twelve in
number. The Deputy Grand Master, Senior Grand Warden and Junior
Grand Warden shall be Trustees ex-officio; Two Trustees shall be
members of the Executive Committee (other than the Grand Master) as
the Grand Master may appoint until the close of the next Annual
Communication of the Grand Lodge. Tthe other nine ten Trustees shall
be appointed by successive Grand Masters. The term of appointive
Trustees shall be three years from the close of the Annual
Communication of Grand Lodge. At all times four five of the nine ten

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Recommendation seeks to give the Grand Master the option of
selecting which members of his Executive Committee shall serve on the several
Constitutional Boards of our Grand Lodge.
At the present, specified members of the Executive Committee are
required by law to serve on the Masonic Homes Board, the California Masonic
Foundation Board, and the California Masonic Memorial Temple Board. Such
inflexibility is at times uneconomical and does not serve those Boards well.
By giving him increased flexibility, this Recommendation will enable
the Grand Master to better discharge the executive functions of Grand Lodge.
Efficiency and economy are not only the policies of most successful
organizations, but of this Grand Lodge as well. This administrative change is
consistent with good administration and present Grand Lodge Policy.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Grand Master Kirkpatrick’s Recommendation No. 2004-05
Candidate’s Contributions to the Masonic Homes, the California Masonic
Memorial Temple and the George Washington Masonic National Memorial
At the present time candidates for the degrees of Masonry in our lodges
– and Master Masons affiliating from outside of California – are required to
make a one-time contribution to the Masonic Homes of California, the California
Masonic Memorial Temple, and the George Washington Masonic National
Memorial. These amounts have remained unchanged for many years. In the
meantime the economy has grown, and the original amounts are now worth far
less than they were when first adopted. It is therefore my recommendation that
candidates for the degrees of Masonry in our lodges, and Master Masons
affiliating from out of state, be required to contribute the following amounts: To
the Masonic Homes of California, $100, to the California Masonic Memorial
Temple, $20, and to the George Washington Masonic National Memorial $10.
The most significant contribution is for our Masonic Homes – which is as it

should be.

CARRY-OVER FROM 2003 ANNUAL COMMUNICATION
Grand Master Holsinger’s Recommendation No. 1
One Day Conferrals
There has been a growing interest in the idea of the conferral of all
three degrees of Ancient Craft Masonry in one day throughout the United States.
Today more than half the Grand Lodges in the United States allow this as an
option to supplement the traditional conferral of the degrees one at a time over a
period of weeks or months. I believe that it is now time for California to join
this movement. I therefore propose that we amend our code to allow the
optional conferral of all three degrees in the same day, but under strict controls,
and only with the permission of the Grand Master.
In order to do this, it will be necessary to amend some portions of the
California Masonic Code to allow for one-day conferrals to occur. The
following changes will have to be made:
1.

The present limit on the number of candidates that can be
accommodated at any one time must be waived.

2.

The present requirement that a candidate memorize the
Obligation, and the Words, Signs and Modes of Recognition
must be waived, along with the mandatory Masonic Education
requirement.

Nothing else needs to be changed, although the legislation as proposed
would allow the Grand Master to waive any pertinent parts of the code necessary
to implement the “one day conferral.” I therefore recommend that the following
language be added to the California Masonic Code which would permit the
conferral of all three degrees in one day on any number of candidates.
If you adopt this recommendation, Section 26280 of the California
Masonic Code will be amended to read as follows and a new Section 26285 will
be added to the California Masonic Code to read as follows:
§26280. RESTRICTIONS ON CONFERRING DEGREES.
Except as provided in Section 26285, nNo Lodge in this Jurisdiction
shall confer:
A.

Any degree on Sunday;

B.

Degrees upon more than five candidates at any one meeting;

C.

More than one degree upon a candidate at any one meeting;

D.

Any of the degrees upon more than one candidate at a time;

174

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

E.

More than five degrees in any one day;

F.

A degree where the Lodge has been called down for the
purpose of examination;

G.

A degree without completing it at the time it is conferred,
except because of an emergency over which Lodge officers
have no control; or

H.

A degree or any part of a degree prior to the time fixed for
convening a meeting therefor.

§26285. ONE DAY CONFERRAL OF DEGREES.
Nothing in the Ritual or the California Masonic Code shall prohibit the
Grand Master from authorizing the conferral of one or more of the
degrees of Freemasonry on any number of candidates at one time at one
or more locations within this Jurisdiction. The proficiency and Masonic
education requirements for those thus initiated, passed and/or raised
shall be determined by the Grand Master.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Holsinger’s carryover Recommendation No. 03-01 would
permit the conferral of all three degrees of Masonry on multiple candidates in a
single day. Grand Master Holsinger believes that it has become necessary to
permit these one-day conferrals to avoid Masonry in California shortly becoming
a thing of the past. Our membership has been in decline for many years. Since
1965, the number of California Masons has dropped by almost 75%. We have a
highly aged membership that is dying off far more rapidly than the pace at which
we currently initiate new candidates. All projections indicate this steep decline
in membership wil continue unabated.
During this past year, the Ritual Committee made five presentations
around the state to show how the one-day conferral would work. Each degree
would be presented in its entirety, including the long-form lecture, by the best
ritualists in the state. There would be one “active” candidate who would be
taken through the degree. Each additional candidate would be accompanied by a
Master Mason who would serve as a mentor. Each candidate would have his
own Holy Writings, square and compass, would take the obligations with his
hands appropriately placed, would learn the steps and signs, and would receive
his apron, just as any other candidate would. Following each degree, there
would be a 45 minute Masonic education presentation. Currently, 34
jurisdictions across the United States permit one-day conferrals.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

175

This Recommendation retains the same safeguards for screening
members as is currently set forth in the California Masonic Code, thus assuring
that only men of good character will be considered for membership in our
fraternity. Under this Recommendation, the proposed candidate still must have
recommenders and references. It will still be the responsibility of the Lodge to
receive and review the application for degrees, investigate the candidate, and
ballot on the petition. Futhermore, it will be up to each Lodge to decide whether
or not to participate in a one-day conferral. Each Lodge will still be free to
confer the degrees in the traditional manner.
This Recommendation represents a change in the current Policy of this
Grand Lodge; however, it is consistent with the General Purposes for which this
Grand Lodge has been established.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
Most Worshipful, the Finance Committee has determined that this
Recommendation, if adopted, could positively affect the Grand Lodge Cudget,
depending upon whether more candidates were Initiated, Passed and Raised,
than would otherwise be the case. We have noted that the proposal is expected
to be self-supporting, and therefore any cost to Grand Lodge would be
negligible.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Brethren of the Grand Lodge, the Committee on Ritual offers the
following report on Grand Master Holsinger’s Recommendation No. 2003-01:
This resolution seeks to allow the optional conferral of all three
Degrees of Masonry in one day, but under strict controls, and then only at the
direction of the Grand Master.
It is the opinion of your Ritual Committee, that Grand Master
Holsinger’s Recommendation No. 2003-01 contains sufficient instruction to
adequately modify the California Masonic Code to allow for the conferral of all
three degrees in one day.
Over the past six months, you have had the opportunity to attend a
demonstration of some of the methods that will need to be employed in a “One
Day Conferral.”
We trust that you learned that the major components of a “One Day
Conferral” program are:
•

Voluntary and Optional Participation

•

Excellent Ritual Teams

176

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

•

Mentor and Masonic Education

Your Ritual Committee is ready to ensure that whatever final decision is
made by this Grand Lodge, the instructions of our Masonic principles will be
maintained at their highest level.
Please remember that the conferral of our Degrees makes a man a
member. What follows thereafter makes the member a Mason.
Report of the Executive Committee:
Most Worshipful Grand Master and Members of Grand Lodge: The
Executive Committee has carefully studied the issue of allowing the use of One
Day Conferrals in California and has made the following observations:
1.

No lodge can be required to participate; it is optional;

2.

No candidate can be required to participate;

3.

It can only take place if the Grand Master is satisfied that the ritual
and Masonic Education are of the highest quality;

4.

It does not replace how we confer degrees; it only supplements it;

5.

Candidates will continue to apply to a properly Chartered Lodge,
where they will be interviewed, investigated and balloted upon in
the current manner;

6.

Lodges maintain control of their candidates;

7.

Lodges have asked for this tool to help them with their candidates;

8.

There are over 7,000 Masons in California who have not advanced
from the Entered Apprentice or Fellow Craft degree within a year
of completing their last degree;

9.

If we are going to hold our membership at its current level, it will
require that every lodge in California raise eleven Master Masons
every year for the next ten years. Many of our lodges can’t do that
without this tool;

10. This recommendation adds appropriate structure to the Grand
Master’s right to make Masons at sight;
11. The conferral of degrees on passive candidates is already allowed
by Section 26320 of the California Masonic Code for impaired
candidates, this recommendation will allow it to be used in One
Day Conferrals as well;

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

177

12. The replacement of proficiencies for the Entered Apprentice and
Fellow Craft Degrees with required Masonic Education only
applies to candidates sent by their lodge to a One Day Conferral;
13. The current requirements to complete the proficiency for the
Master Mason degree remain the same;
14. The required Masonic Education will include material that explains
the symbolism in each of the degrees and the candidates will be
able to take the material home to study;
15. The Masonic Education includes the questions and answers
required of all candidates, the proper method of communicating the
pass and words as well as instruction in signs and proper modes of
recognition.
When all of these facts are taken into consideration, how can we not
allow those lodges that need this tool to keep their lodges alive? The
requirement to repeat a lengthy proficiency to advance to the next degree is not a
landmark in Freemasonry, in fact there are many Grand Lodges in the United
States and in Europe where the only requirement to advance is the answering of
a series of questions in open lodge. This recommendation replaces the
proficiencies in the first and second degree for One Day Conferral Candidates
with significant Masonic Education designed to not only teach the values of
Freemasonry, but also to instill in the candidates a commitment to life long
learning and self improvement that is a hallmark of a Mason. Even if your lodge
does not want to make use of this tool, why would you deny another lodge
asking for this assistance? Your vote for this recommendation is a vote to help
lodges that need to use this tool for their candidates.
Because no lodge or candidate can be required to participate in a One
Day Conferral, because it requires significant Masonic Education, because it can
only be used to supplement and not replace our current method of making
Masons and because the Grand Master will insure that it is only used when
appropriate, the Executive Committee recommends a Yes vote on Grand Master
Holsinger’s Recommendation No. 1.
Recommendation NOT ADOPTED.
Grand Master Holsinger’s Recommendation No. 2
Flexible Dates for Annual Communication
Section 410 of the California Masonic Code specifies that the Annual
Communication of this Grand Lodge shall always commence on the second
Monday in October. The inflexibility of this arrangement means that we have no
choice in negotiating better rates for hotels when other major citywide events

178

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

conflict with our Annual Communication, such as Fleet Week. In addition, in
many years our Annual Communication occurs at the same time as the
observance of Yom Kippur, creating a burden on our members of the Jewish
faith who must choose between the Annual Communication and their religious
observance. It is therefore my recommendation that we amend the California
Masonic Code to allow the holding of the Annual Communication on a specific
day within the twenty days of the second Monday in October as may be selected
by the Grand Master at least one year in advance. Such advance notice will give
ample opportunity to make hotel reservations well before the scheduled date.
If you adopt this recommendation, Section 410 of the California
Masonic Code will be amended to read as follows:
§410. ANNUAL.
Grand Lodge shall hold an Annual Communication for the transaction
of its regular business commencing at 9:00 a.m. on the second Monday
of each October, in the City and County of San Francisco. commencing
at a time and on a day within twenty (20) days before or after the
second Monday in October, as selected and announced by the Grand
Master at least one year in advance.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Grand Master Holsinger’s carryover Recommendation No. 03-02 seeks
to establish flexible dates, rather than a fixed date, for the Annual
Communication of this Grand Lodge.
Although the date of our Annual Communication has changed from
time to time, it has always been held on a fixed date. From 1864 to 1955, this
Grand Lodge met on the second Monday of October. From 1956 to 1961, the
date of the Annual Communication was changed to the last week in September.
At the Annual Communication of 1961, Grand Master Breslauer
recommended that the Annual Communication be changed back to its original
date, as established in 1864, to the second Monday in October. He noted in his
recommendation that those Brethren engaged in agriculture and those whose
business required their attention in the area of bookkeeping and accounting
found the end of September to be most inconvenient to them. He also noted that
there will always be someone who will be inconvenienced by the time and dated
fixed for the Annual Communication. His recommendation was adopted by a
5/6 vote.
In 1996, a proposed resolution changing the date of the Annual
Communication to the third week in October was defeated.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

179

The Policy of this Grand Lodge has always been to establish a date
certain for our Annual Communication so delegates can properly calendar their
activities. This Recommendation proposes flexible meeting dates, dates that
may change from year to year, contrary to long standing Policy.
This Recommendation is not consistent with the Policy and General
Purposes of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
Most Worshipful, this Recommendation will allow for a much greater
flexibility in determining the timing of the Annual Communication of Grand
Lodge, and has the potential for savings on housing costs for the officers and
guests of Grand Lodge, as well as for the representatives and members of our
lodges. Both the Fairmont Hotel and the Holiday Inn are business traveler
hotels, and have indicated potential lower rates for weekends, and have
expressed their willingness to discuss the room rates. However, the actual rates
or savings may vary, depending, among other factors, on the scheduling of other
larger conventions in the city or at these hotels.
Report of the Executive Committee:
Most Worshipful Sirs, Brethren of Grand Lodge: Grand Master
Holsinger’s Recommendation No. 2 would permit the Grand Master on at least
one-year advance notice to the Brethren to change the date for conducting the
Annual Communication within a thirty day window of the present mandatory
second Monday of October.
If passed, it has been agreed, that the first year of change would not
occur until October 2007, which would permit adequate notice to all concerned.
The underlying purpose of this recommendation is to seek increased
attendance at our Annual Communication by giving the Grand Master the option
to hold the session on a weekend rather than opening on Monday at the
beginning of the work week. The number of Masters and Wardens attending has
been steadily decreasing with most absentees advising that they had work
commitments as more and more Lodge officers are no longer among the retired.
Your Research and Planning Committee was asked to study and
rendered a favorable report on the feasibility of such a change and how the
Annual Communication program might be conducted. At the 2003 Annual
Communication, this Recommendation was approved by 67.6% of the voting
membership.
There may also be a potential cost savings by conducting the Annual
Session on a weekend from Friday through Sunday.

180

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Other appendant and concordant bodies scheduling, particularly the
Order of the Eastern Star, will be taken into consideration.
It should be noted that this Recommendation has nothing to do with
religious holidays, as such holidays fall on every day of the week, depending
upon the year.
Recommendation ADOPTED.
Resolution 03-02
Reduces Stated Meeting Quorum from Seven to Five
The proponents of this Resolution seek to reduce the quorum required for Lodge
Stated Meetings from seven to five members.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, most of the Lodges in this Jurisdiction are experiencing a
decline in membership; and
WHEREAS, approximately twenty percent of the Lodges in this
Jurisdiction have 100 members or less; and
WHEREAS, many of these smaller Lodges close or consolidate each
year; and
WHEREAS, it is a benefit to Masonry to keep these small Lodges
open; and
WHEREAS, many of these Lodges cannot conduct business for lack of
a quorum.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that Section 16010 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§16010. CONDITIONS FOR CONDUCTING AND BUSINESS
TO BE TRANSACTED.
A Lodge shall not be opened, nor shall any work be done or business
transacted, except by, or in the presence of, and under the continual
direction of the Master or the ranking Warden or the Grand Master or
his designated representative. All business, except the examination of
candidates in the lectures of the Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft
degrees, the conferring of the Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft
degrees, and the consideration of relief of a worthy Brother, shall be
done in a Lodge of Master Masons. No Lodge shall transact any
business with less than seven five members present except the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

181

conferring of degrees. The Tiler is present while at his post in the
Tiler’s room.
All business shall be transacted at a Stated Meeting, except the
following may also be conducted at a Special Meeting:
A.

Collections or appropriations for charity;

B.

Conferring degrees;

C.

Ceremonial observances;

D.

Electing a representative to the Grand Lodge;

E.

Balloting for commissioners to try charges of unmasonic
conduct; or

F.

After dispensation from the Grand Master:
1.

Balloting for and conferring the degrees upon an
applicant without reference to a committee, or

2.

Receiving and acting upon the application of a
rejected applicant within less than 12 months after the
date of his rejection.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of carryover Resolution No. 03-02 seek to reduce the
quorum required for a Lodge Stated Meeting from seven to five. They cite our
declining membership as a primary reason that many Lodges cannot conduct
business for lack of a quorum.
Research reveals that the real reason for the quorum of seven is from
our ritual. Entered Apprentice Lodges consisted of not les than seven, and until
about 1843, Lodges in this country conducted their Stated Meetings on the First
Degree.
It has been the Policy of the Grand Lodge of California, since its
formation in 1850, to required Stated Meetings to be conducted in a Lodge of
Master Masons. This reflects the practice which emerged after the Baltimore
Conference of 1843 when all Grand Lodges were urged to require their Lodges
to conduct business on the Third Degree. The theory of the day was that an
imposter would have to memorize three times as much ritual in order to gain
admission into a Stated Meeting. However,t he ritual was not changed to reflect
this innovation in Masonry, and we still keep the Entered Apprentice quorum of
seven for our Stated Meetings, thus reflecting our roots in the Grand Lodges
from which our first five lodges came.

182

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

This Resolution is a change that is not consistent with current Grand
Lodge Policy.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Most Worshipful, Brethren of the Grand Lodge, the Committee on
Ritual offers the following report on the Carry-over Resolution No. 2003-02:
This resolution seeks to reduce the quorum of members of a Lodge
needed to conduct business from seven to five.
The long-established minimum Ritual requirements for opening a
Lodge of Master Masons in due form call for seven positions to be filled.
This resolution calls for changing one word in one sentence of
California Masonic Code Section 16010. The proponents want the code to read:
“No Lodge shall transact any business with less than five members present
except the conferring of degrees.” The resolution does not change the longestablished Ritual requirement for opening a Lodge of Master Mason in
due form. Therefore, if this resolution passes, a Lodge would still require seven
ritually qualified Master Masons to be present.
Resolution ADOPTED.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

2004 RESOLUTIONS
Resolution No. 04-01
Re-establishes Powers of the Hall Associations
The proponents of this Resolution seek to reestablish the Lodges of this
jurisdiction as the managers of their own business decisions and their own
Temple Associations.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 02-07 “Requires Temple Associations for
Real Property” was necessary from the perspective of protecting lodges from
certain liability risks;
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 02-07 went too far in that it deprives the
constituent lodges of their autonomy and their own decision management;
WHEREAS, Temple Associations and lodges are closer to the ongoing
actions than Grand Lodge Committees;
WHEREAS, Temple Associations have a more intimate knowledge of
details;
WHEREAS, the Grand Lodge Committee on Masonic Properties is too
remote from the actual ongoing status and does not have the intimate knowledge
of the ongoing activities;
WHEREAS, the Temple Associations are still responsible to request
advice and assistance from the Committee on Masonic Properties; and
WHEREAS, this Resolution would return appropriate autonomy to the
constituent Lodges.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 6050 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§6050. MASONIC PROPERTIES.
The Committee on Masonic Properties shall examine, and approve all
details review and prepare a written advisement report to any requesting
Lodge or Temple Association regarding any contract involving a
Lodge, Temple Association, committee, partnership or corporation or
other entity representing itself to be Masonic in character, or operating
for the benefit of a Lodge or Lodges in this Jurisdiction for:
A.

Any option or contract for the purchase of real property, the
encumbrance of real property or the lease of real property, as
lessor or lessee, for more than three years;

184

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

B.

A contract for or commencement of the construction,
alteration, improvement, replacement, repair, or maintenance
of a Masonic Temple in excess of $25,000; and

C.

Any option or contract regarding the sale or listing for sale of
any real property owned by such entity.

The committee shall, subject to the approval of the Grand Master,
establish rules and regulations governing the processing of applications
for committee approval.
The Committee on Masonic Properties shall complete the written
advisement report within 60 days after receipt of a written request for
advisement and forward it to the entity making the request. Lodges and
Temple Associations are not obliged to act on the recommendations of
the Committee on Masonic Properties.
The committee shall review the reports of transactions of Temple
Associations submitted to the Grand Secretary as provided for
elsewhere in this Code and may give counsel and advise concerning the
operation of those Temple Associations.
The committee shall, also, advise Lodges and the Grand Lodge on
matters of taxation with respect to income, property, sales and
employment taxes.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 21010 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§21010. CONTROL AND USE.
A Lodge must retain control of its own funds and property. It may
deposit its funds in a sound and reputable bank or banks. A Chartered
Lodge may, after receiving consent with assistance from the Grand
Lodge Committee on Masonic Properties, cause the incorporation of a
Temple Association for the purpose of purchasing, erecting or
maintaining and holding title to real estate for a temple for Lodge uses
and purposes, and a Lodge or Lodges may hold shares of stock in such
corporation. Space in such temple may be rented for commercial and
other uses consistent with the purposes of the Lodge or the corporation
and not in violation of this Code.
A Lodge shall not incorporate. It shall not divide its funds or property
with its members, even those withdrawing to form a new Lodge or with
the new Lodge thus formed. A Lodge shall not divide its funds with an
existing Lodge by way of a contribution.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

185

A Lodge shall adopt a budget for the following twelve months at the
first Stated Meeting held after the annual installation of the officers of
the Lodge.
No money shall be drawn without the express consent of the Lodge,
even though included in a Lodge budget, except that:
A.

Dispensed by the Charity Committee;

B.

Due Grand Lodge; and

C.

Required to pay the taxes or other charges of local, State or
Federal governmental agencies.

Lodges may approve bills against the Lodge only when the bills are
supported by proper documentation, such as an invoice or receipt. No
warrant shall be drawn unless there are sufficient funds to honor it.
A Lodge shall not invest any of its funds in any manner or obligate
itself in any way for the purpose of acquiring land or building a
Masonic temple except upon compliance with the provisions of this
Code.
A Lodge shall not establish or form a foundation separate from the
Lodge with power to receive, hold, administer, invest or reinvest funds
received by the Lodge.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 22010 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§22010. ORGANIZATION AND OPERATION.
All Lodges shall use an incorporated Temple Association for the
purpose of purchasing, erecting or maintaining and holding title to real
estate for a temple for Lodge uses and purposes, and a Lodge or Lodges
may hold shares of stock in such corporation. Lodges shall seek and
receive the consent advice of the Grand Lodge Committee on Temple
Properties and Taxation Masonic Properties before establishing such
corporation, and may reduce the annual per capita payment to Grand
Lodge by the amount of the California corporation filing fee(s) incurred
in establishing and maintaining such corporation. Lodges with such
existing corporations shall seek review by the Grand Lodge Committee
on Temple Properties and Taxation Masonic Properties of its corporate
status and the extent to which that status shields Lodge members from
liability, to the extent permissible by California State law.

186

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

All Temple Associations shall be organized and shall operate as
follows:
A.

A Temple Association may be organized either as a non-profit
or as a general law corporation under the laws of the State in
which it owns or is to acquire real estate, land or a building;

B.

The exclusive purpose for which a Temple Association may be
organized is to hold title to property., collect income therefrom
and turn over the entire amount thereof, less expenses, to its
members or shareholders, all of which must be organizations
described in The income less expenses therefrom shall be
used for the advancement of Lodge uses, purposes and
solvency. A Temple Association must conduct its activities in
accordance with Section 501(c) of the current Internal
Revenue Code of 1954. A Temple Association shall not
engage in any activities or exercise any powers that are not in
furtherance of this exclusive purpose.
The Temple
Association may accumulate funds for the purpose of
providing maintenance, repairs, upgrades, improvements and
expansion to its properties. Such funds shall be maintained in
and as a separately identified fund. No part of the net earnings
of a Temple Association shall inure to the benefit of any
member or private shareholder, as defined for purposes of
Section 501(c)(7) of the current Internal Revenue Code of
1954;

C.

Uniform Articles of Incorporation and By-Laws for Temple
Associations written by the Grand Lodge Committee on
Masonic Properties, with the approval of the Grand Master,
shall may be adopted, in whole or in part, for use by each
Temple Association. Any amendments to such Uniform
Articles or By-laws by the Grand Lodge Committee on
Masonic Properties, with the approval of the Grand Master,
also shall may also be adopted for use by each Temple
Association. Appropriate revisions to the Uniform Articles
and By-laws may be authorized by the Committee on Masonic
Properties for Temple Associations with more than one
member or shareholder;

D.

A Lodge shall exercise its rights as a member or shareholder of
a Temple Association, including its right to elect or remove
directors, pursuant only to the authorization of a majority of
members of the Lodge present at a Stated Meeting of the
Lodge at which a quorum is present. Such authorization shall
not be contested if written instruments, executed by the Master
or Secretary of the Lodge, setting forth the authorization are

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

187

filed with the Secretary of the Temple Association. The Lodge
may exercise its rights through the Master of the Lodge, or any
person authorized by proxy or power of attorney duly executed
by him who is authorized to execute any documents and to
vote the Lodge’s membership or shares at any members’ or
shareholders’ meeting of the Temple Association in
accordance with the terms of any action previously authorized
by the Lodge;
E.

A Temple Association shall submit a written report to each
Lodge which is a member or shareholder of the Association on
August 1 of each year and on the following February 1. The
report shall be presented at the next Stated Meeting of the
Lodge. The August 1 report shall include a statement of
income and expenditures for the six month period ending on
the preceding June 30, together with a statement of the assets
and liabilities of the Association on the preceding June 30.
The February 1 report shall include a statement of income and
expenditures for the twelve month period ending on the
preceding December 31, together with a statement of the assets
and liabilities of the Association on the preceding December
31. A copy of each such report shall be given to the Inspector
of the District to which such Lodge is assigned;

F.

Annually on or before the 15th day of the fifth month
following the end of its fiscal year, each Temple Association
shall submit to the Grand Secretary a full and correct report of
its transactions for that preceding fiscal year on a form as may
be provided. The report shall include a statement of assets,
liabilities and net worth as of the end of the 12 month period.
Temple Associations formed during the 12 month period shall
transmit a report from the date of incorporation;

G.

A Temple Association may not hold or administer funds for
any purpose not specifically set forth in this Code; and

H.

Temple Associations funds, not Lodge funds, must be used to
compensate employees.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
Resolution No. 04-01 seeks to reestablish the constituent lodges of this
jurisdiction as the managers of their own business decisions and their own
Temple Associations without oversight by Grand Lodge. This Resolution would
be a reversal of policy in recent years in which the Grand Lodge has assisted
with the operation of the Building Associations of the various Lodges throughout
this jurisdiction.
By this Resolution, the Temple Association would be able to retain any
money that it receives. That is contrary to the current procedure, which requires
the Temple Association to distribute all money to the Lodge, except for certain
contingencies and a specific reserve for expenses. This Resolution would also

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

189

remove all safeguards provided by the Grand Lodge Uniform Bylaws, by
permitting the Temple Associations to simply ignore them.
The proposed Resolution would be an erosion of the leadership of the
lodge by permitting the Temple Association, under the idea of “home rule”, to
run “roughshod” over the Lodge without the attention, supervision and in some
instances, regretfully, protection offered by the Grand Lodge Committee on
Masonic Properties.
This Resolution is not consistent with the Policy and General Purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Masonic Properties:
Most Worshipful Grand Master and members of Grand Lodge. In
reviewing the proposed Resolution No. 04-01 I have a concern that this
Resolution will take away all of the years of work that the Masonic Properties
Committee members have undertaken to make it easier on the Hall Associations
in this jurisdiction to understand all of the different concerns that lie out there
when they look forward to handling the everyday operation with insurance, labor
concerns, and reporting, rentals, maintenance, tax, IRS reporting and the
requirements of our Hall Associations throughout the state. Some of the Hall
Associations are not even current with the requirements of this Grand Lodge.
We, the Masonic Properties Committee, have had the opportunity to
observe the workings and concerns of the Hall Associations on almost a day-today basis. A week does not go by that we don’t receive a call concerning some
aspect of the operation of the Hall Association because the directors do not have
the knowledge to make the decisions that are required without some guidance on
the part of the Grand Lodge Committee.
We do give advice to all Hall Associations and ask them to follow the
established rules set down in the California Masonic Code. We ask that they
follow those rules as they go about the task that they have been charged with,
that is the management of the Masonic Hall. The brothers in the Lodge have
elected them to the responsibility of handling the everyday affairs of the building
to the best of their ability and stay within the laws of the Grand Lodge, the State
and local government, and do so in a professional manner, and if they need
assistance, they have a committee of the Grand Lodge to assist them in their
decision making process.
My brothers, we have too much at stake to forfeit the work the Masonic
Properties and Hall Associations have done to make the job of running your Hall
Associations as easy as we can. We are here for the asking, calling, e-mailing or
writing, and we will, and do and will respond in a timely manner, our committee
is scattered about the state, check the Grand Lodge roster for the member nearest
you, give us a try, won’t you?

190

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

As a note, the management classes put on this past year have been a real
eye opener for those attending, put the dates on your calendar for 2005, and
attend the Hall Association program and you will go away much the better for
attending.
I urge you to vote no on this Resolution.
Report of the Executive Committee:
Resolution No. 04-01 removes the Grand Lodge Committee on
Masonic Properties from any involvement with business decisions of any Hall
Association in the Jurisdiction and may even go so far as to remove the authority
of the Grand Master to approve any contractual relationships over $25,000
enacted by a Hall Association. The Masonic Properties Committee and Grand
Master must presently approve these contracts. Hall Associations would not
have to seek this approval and could commit the Lodge to expenditures that it
could not afford. It removes the Grand Lodge Committee from any review of
such contracts and therefore obligates a Lodge to significant debt without proper
review and approval.
If this Resolution were to pass, it is conceivable that the Lodge would
once again become subservient to the Hall Association. It is quite possible for
the Hall Association to be able to accumulate all it’s earnings in a separate
account not reportable to the Lodge. It allows the Hall Association to become a
very independent Board without control from the owners of the facility and to
whom the Board must now report. There have been incidences in this
Jurisdiction where the oversight of the Hall Associations could lead to
unscrupulous business practices wherein the Hall Association Board of Directors
would be personally responsible to the actions of a few and subject to personal
jeopardy. Also, unless adopted under a new set of By-Laws of the Hall
Association, it is quite possible that none of the Officers of the Lodge would
serve on the Board as is now the case.
Without the authority of the Grand Master to regulate the Hall
Associations, it is conceivable that the Hall Association could abuse its
prerogatives and allow uses that could become very embarrassing to
Freemasonry in California such as happened very recently in the Grand Lodge
Jurisdiction of New York.
The Executive Committee recommends a No vote on Resolution No.
04-01.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Resolution No. 04-02
Changes Time for Voting on Grand Lodge Budget
The proponents of this Resolution seek to establish a fixed time for the
consideration and adoption of the Grand Lodge Budget.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, one of the greatest responsibilities of the voting members
of this Grand Lodge is, or should be, the approval of the Grand Lodge Budget;
WHEREAS, the adoption of the Grand Lodge Budget has become a
perfunctory activity in the last hours of the business of the Annual
Communication; and
WHEREAS, consideration and adoption of the Grand Lodge Budget at
a fixed time earlier in the Annual Communication would draw attention to its
importance and likely encourage more careful consideration by more voting
members in attendance at each Annual Communication.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 7040 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§7040. GRAND LODGE BUDGET.
Grand Lodge shall have a budget for each fiscal year as follows:
A.

The Grand Lodge Budget shall be prepared under the direction
and control of the Deputy Grand Master. The Grand Treasurer
and the Grand Lodge Committee on Finance shall consult in
the preparation of the Grand Lodge Budget as requested by the
Deputy Grand Master. The Deputy Grand Master shall be
assisted in the preparation of the budget by such other persons
as he may select to assist him.

B.

Each Grand Lodge Budget shall be based upon the per capita
sum to be collected for Master Masons carried on the rolls of
each Constituent Lodge as of the June 30th most immediately
preceding the commencement of the fiscal year covered by
such budget.

C.

The proposed Grand Lodge Budget shall be transmitted by the
Grand Secretary to the Masters of the several Lodges,
Inspectors and members of the Committee on Finance at least
30 days prior to the commencement date of the next Annual
Communication.

192

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

D.

The proposed Grand Lodge Budget shall be referred to the
Committee on Finance for its review and report to Grand
Lodge before the vote is taken whether to adopt the budget.

E.

The Deputy Grand Master shall present the proposed budget to
the members of Grand Lodge for their approval as a special
order of business taking precedence over all other matters at
9:15 a.m. on the second day of each Annual Communication.
Once the time has arrived for the consideration of the Grand
Lodge Budget, no other business may de conducted until the
budget has been adopted. The Grand Lodge Budget shall be
adopted by a majority vote.

F.

The Grand Treasurer and the Committee on Finance shall
monitor compliance with the adopted Grand Lodge Budget
during the Grand Lodge year.

G.

There shall be three expense categories in the Grand Lodge
Budget:
fraternal, appropriations and general and
administrative. An expense category in the Grand Lodge
Budget shall not be exceeded without the consent of the Grand
Master. An expense category shall not be exceeded by more
than five percent without the consent of at least two of the
following: the Grand Secretary, the Grand Treasurer and the
Chairman of the Committee on Finance.

H.

No appropriation of money shall be made at any Annual
Communication after the election of Grand Officers, except by
a three-fourths vote of the members present.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
This Resolution seeks to establish a specific time during the Annual
Communication for adoption of the Grand Lodge budget, by advancing balloting
on the budget from its current place at the end of the ballot, to the first item to be
voted on, on Tuesday at 9:15 a.m. The stated justification for this proposal is
that because the budget is more important than any other legislation voted on, it
should not be placed as the next to the last item on the agenda.
It is precisely because the budget is so important that it is set for vote at
the end of other business. The policy of voting on the budget at the end of the
ballot, is so that the budget may be adjusted in the event emergent legislation
requires budget adjustment. The present policy of voting on the budget at the
end of the ballot has served this Grand Lodge well and allows for good fiscal
planning.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

193

This Resolution is not consistent with the Policy and General Purposes
of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Executive Committee:
Most Worshipful Grand Master and Members of Grand Lodge: The
Executive Committee of Grand Lodge has reviewed Resolution No. 04-02 and
would like to bring to your attention several matters as it relates to this
Resolution.
The first concern is that it establishes that the Annual Communication
of Grand Lodge must last at least two days without consideration that our
business may be conducted in a shorter period of time depending on the amount
of business before us and how efficient we become in the future. At the present
time, there is no requirement on the number of days the Annual Communication
must contain.
The second area of concern is that there could well be other pieces of
financial legslation on the agenda that might affect the Grand Lodge Budget,
however with the passage of this Resolution would eliminate the possibility to
discuss and pass legislation which could have a positive impact on the budget
before it was adopted.
The Executive Committee urges a No vote on Resolution No. 04-02.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 04-03
Permits Advertising and Public Relations by Lodges
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow Lodges to publish information
to the public regarding Freemasonry and Lodge activities.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 15115 of the California Masonic Code
concerning publications permits in sub-section D a Lodge to publish in a
newspaper of general circulation, an announcement showing the Masonic
emblem, the name and number of the Lodge and the dates of meetings;
WHEREAS, Lodges in this jurisdiction have been running paid
advertisements in newspapers of general circulation extolling the virtues of
Masonry in general and their individual Lodge in particular;
WHEREAS, running of these paid advertisements puts smaller Lodges
with limited funds at a disadvantage because they cannot afford to run similar
advertisements;

194

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

WHEREAS, although this practice seemed to be in violation of the
limitations and spirit imposed by Section 15115D, Grand Master Holsinger at
the 2003 Annual Communication in his Decision No. 1, ruled that “Section
15115 of the California Masonic Code pertains only to the subjects contained in
that section, namely publications pertaining to the business of the Lodge, and not
to a Lodge public relations program which includes informing the public about
Freemasonry in general, and the activities of the Lodge in particular”;
WHEREAS, Grand Master Holsinger’s Decision was approved by
members of the Grand Lodge and will be included in the appendix of the
California Masonic Code; and
WHEREAS, the Decision does not amend the California Masonic
Code nor does it offer any guidelines relative to the content of the
advertisements, the effect of distribution of the advertisements in areas where
other lodges are located, the manner of funding of the advertisements, etc.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 15115 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§15115. PUBLICATIONS.
A Lodge may:
A.

Publish and mail a Lodge bulletin informing the members of
the Lodge of its activities and including news items relating to
other Masonic organizations such as to promote Masonic
intercourse. The bulletin may be a joint publication of the
Lodge and one or more other Lodges and Masonic
Organizations if each participating organization pays its
prorata share of the cost to publish and mail the bulletin.
However, the sponsors of a joint publication may permit any
Masonic Youth Order to include material in the publication
without charge, and the sponsors may assume the cost of
mailing the publication to members of the Masonic Youth
Order;

B.

Post at the entrance of the community where the Lodge is
located, a sign or plaque exhibiting the Masonic emblem and
giving the name and number of the Lodge and information as
to when and where it meets;

C.

Publish a roster for Masonic purposes and for the benefit of its
members containing the names, home addresses and home
telephone numbers of all members, including Entered
Apprentices and Fellow Craft, together with the business or

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

195

vocation, business address and business telephone number of
each member; and
D.

Publish in a newspaper of general circulation an announcement
showing the Masonic emblem, the name and number of the
Lodge and the dates of meetings. A Lodge may also publish
advertisements in a newspaper of general circulation relative
to a Lodge’s public relations program that may inform the
public about Freemasonry in general and the activities of the
Lodge in particular, provided that:
1.

The advertisements do not violate Masonic law
relative to solicitation of members;

2.

Funds used to pay for such advertisements comply
with the requirements of all applicable sections of
fund expenditures as provided for elsewhere in this
Code; and

3.

Such advertisements do not promote one Lodge as
being more important or desirable than another Lodge
or other Lodges located in the area of publication.

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to clarify Section 15115 D of
the California Masonic Code regarding what is permissible advertising in a
public newspaper. This Resolution would add the following language to that
Code section:
A lodge may also publish advertisements in a newspaper of general
circulation relative to a Lodge’s public relations program that may
inform the public about Freemasonry in general and the activities of the
Lodge in particular provided that:
1.

The advertisements do not violate Masonic law relative
to solicitation of members;

2.

Funds used to pay for such advertisements comply with
all of the requirements of Sections 21080 and 21090 of
the California Masonic Code; and

3.

That such advertisements do not promote one lodge at the
expense of another lodge or lodges located in the area of
publication.

There has been confusion as to whether Section 15115, as it is currently
written, permits this type of dissemination of information. For a number of
years, this Grand Lodge has recognized that the “outside world” knows little

196

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

about the fraternity of Free and Accepted Masons or its appendant organizations.
The general public often does not understand the philosophy of Freemasonry in
assisting not only members of our fraternity, but also members of the community
in many aspects of daily life. Newspaper ads, ads in magazines, and information
in local newspapers and periodicals have often times been used as public
relations tools to inform non-masons of the role in our communities played by
Grand Lodge, local Lodges, or one of our auxiliary organizations. This
Resolution conforms to and makes clear the policy of our Grand Lodge of
informing the public about Freemasonry in our communities.
This Resolution is consistent with the Policy and General Purposes of
this Grand Lodge.
Resolution ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 04-04
Requires Flag Salute at All Lodge Meetings
The proponents of this Resolution seek to require the pledge of allegiance to the
flag of the United States at every Lodge meeting.
To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, we are enjoined to be exemplary in the discharge of our
civic duties and patriotism is a civic duty;
WHEREAS, the Lodges of this jurisdiction regularly salute the Flag of
the United States of America at almost every event we attend, such as Hall
Association meetings, dinners, etc.;
WHEREAS, the concordant bodies in this jurisdiction all have the Flag
salute incorporated into their opening ritual; and
WHEREAS, the Pledge of Allegiance to the Flag of the United States
of America is omitted at degree nights because it is not a part of the opening
ritual.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that Section 16100 of the
California Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§16100. DISPLAY OF FLAG.
The Flag of our country shall be displayed and the pledge of allegiance
recited at all meetings of Lodges, and the Flag shall be carried on all
ceremonial occasions except at funerals. At the annual election of
officers, and at such other meetings as may be deemed desirable by the

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

197

Master, the Flag shall be formally presented at the altar and received in
the manner prescribed by the Grand Lecturer.
The Altar of a Lodge shall not be decorated with any flag.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The Pledge of Allegiance is generally regarded as a statement reflecting
one’s patriotism for this country. The proponents of this Resolution wish to
make the Pledge a mandatory component of every tiled meeting in every Lodge
in California.
There is no uniform policy regarding the occasions when the Pledge is
recited in any jurisdiction in the United States. In California, Section 16100 of
the California Masonic Code requires that the flag be displayed at certain
specified occasions. Under our current practice it is the prerogative of the
Master of any Lodge to have the Pledge recited at other occasions that he
believes to be appropriate. If he so chooses, the Pledge could be recited at every
meeting. Nothing in our law prevents an individual Lodge from adopting such a
tradition if it so desires.
Words alone do not create patriotism, and patriotism does not need
designated times for expression. This Resolution would only create an
unnecessary requirement for those Lodges and Masons who would otherwise
choose to express their love of country at a different time or place. It should be
noted that this proposal has been made before; 1934, 1939, 1964, 1968, 1972,
1986, 1990, 1991 and 2002. All of them failed.
This Resolution is not consistent with the policy of this Grand Lodge.
Report of the Committee on Ritual:
Most Worshipful, Brethen of the Grand Lodge, the Committee on
Ritual offers the following report on the Resolution No. 2004-04: (Requires flag
salute at all Lodge Meetings)
The proponents of this Resolution seek to require the Flag Salute be
recited at all Lodge Meetings and the flag carried on all ceremonial occasions,
except funerals.
It should be noted that since 1927, reciting the pledgeo f allegiance to
the Flag of our Country has been an option available to the Lodge Master. The
Ritual Committee sees no problems with this portion of the resolution making
the recitation a requirement, and if adopted will determine its proper
implementation.
Additionally, California Masonic Code Section 16100
currently requires the flag to be carried at all ceremonies, except funerals.

198

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

Therefore, the Ritual Committee sees no problem with the latter part of the
resolution.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 23090 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§23090. ENTERED APPRENTICES AND FELLOW CRAFTS.
Entered Apprentices and Fellow Crafts have only the right to sit in the
Lodge in which they received the degree or degrees when open in a
degree taken by them, and the right to advancement therein after
satisfactorily passing a strict examination in open Lodge. They may,
with the consent of the Master, visit another Lodge working in a degree
taken by them, if accompanied by a Master Mason who has sat with
them in open Lodge and vouches for them.
They are not required to pay dues and Entered Apprentices and Fellow
Crafts shall pay dues in like manner as Master Masons, but they are not
entitled to vote or speak in Lodge. All provisions of this Code
applicable to dues (such as dues remission and suspension for
nonpayment of dues) shall apply to Entered Apprentices and to Fellow
Crafts in the same manner as to Master Masons. They Entered
Apprentices and Fellow Crafts may be buried with Masonic honors
upon the request of their family. Neither they nor their widows or
orphans are entitled to relief from the Lodge. They shall not exercise
any control over Lodge funds for charity, or for other purposes.
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that Section 27010 of the California
Masonic Code be amended as marked:
§27010. MINIMUM.
The minimum annual dues are $12. Dues shall commence with the first
day of the month next following the conferring of the Master Mason
Entered Apprentice Degree or as provided for elsewhere in this Code.

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

201

Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of this Resolution seek to amend the California
Masonic Code to require Entered Apprentice and Fellow Craft Masons to pay
annual dues to their lodges. They assert that since they are increasingly
becoming part of the life of each lodge they are creating a burden on the brethren
and the Lodge by participating in activities, and on Grand Lodge itself by not
paying their fair share of expenses.
Entered Apprentices and Fellow Crafts have no voice in the government
of their lodge. They cannot attend Stated Meetings or vote. At the end of the
Third Degree, the Master tells the newly made Master Mason that the only thing
remaining to constitute him a member of the Lodge is to sign its by-laws. This
clearly affirms that until then he was never considered a member of his Lodge
and was not supposed to be bearing any of the Lodge’s financial burden.
This Resolution is not consistent with the Policy and General Purposes
of this Grand Lodge of California.
Report of the Committee on Finance:
Most Worshipful, this Resolution, if adopted, would result in additional
per capita income to Grand Lodge, depending upon the number of Entered
Apprentices and Fellow Crafts who pay dues.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.
Resolution No. 04-06
Withdrawn
Resolutions No. 04-07, 04-08, 04-09, 04-10
Declared out of order because they did not have the necessary qualified
proponents. Resolutions must have sponsors from three members of Grand
Lodge each of whom is from a different Lodge. These resolutions did not
qualify in that respect, and have therefore been declared out of order.
Resolution No. 04-11
Allows California Masons to Belong to the Ancient and
Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot
The proponents of this Resolution seek to allow California Masons to belong to
the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot.

202

PROCEEDINGS OF THE

To the Most Worshipful Grand Lodge, F.& A.M. of California:
WHEREAS, Section 15130 of the California Masonic Code requires
that “A Mason residing in this jurisdiction shall not become a member of any
Masonic Organization unless: A. The Masonic Organization existed in the
Jurisdiction on September 27, 1957; or B. The Constitution and By-Laws of the
Masonic Organization have first been submitted to Grand Lodge for approval at
a subsequent Annual Communication. Any organization so approved shall
conduct its business and activities in conformance with the laws of Grand
Lodge.”;
WHEREAS, Section 19 of the California Masonic Code defines
“Masonic Organizations” as “Any group, chapter, order, club, association or
organization requiring Masonic affiliation as a prerequisite to membership,
except Masonic Lodges.”;
WHEREAS, the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot is a
Masonic Organization open to Master Masons in good standing that was
instituted in Germany in about 1960 by Bro. Varick Steele, PGHP of the Grand
Chapter, RAM of Germany, based on his historical research related to Alexander
the Great of Macedonia and involves Alexander’s journey to Gordium and his
loosing of the famed Gordian Knot;
WHEREAS, the ritual of the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian
Knot is calculated to demonstrate the inherent unity of all regular Masons, in all
walks of life and in all nations of the world;
WHEREAS, the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot is
itself international in nature, it having instituted bodies, styled Synods, in
Germany, Dharan, Saudia Arabia and a now defunct body in Hollywood,
California;
WHEREAS, the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot meets
in Synods only for the purpose of conferring the one degree controlled by the
Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot;
WHEREAS, each Synod of the Ancient and Heroic Order of the
Gordian Knot utilizes just four officers to confer the Order’s one degree, which
officers have no other duty except the performance of the ritual;
WHEREAS, Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot admits
members only as life membership and whereas life membership costs as little as
$10;
WHEREAS, outside of Freemasonry, interest in Alexander the Great
has increased in recent years following the documentary series, In the Footsteps
of Alexander, and has movie producers Baz Luhrman (Moulin Rouge, etc.) and
Oliver Stone (Platoon, etc.) to each undertake major motion pictures, currently
under production, relating to Alexander the Great; and

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

203

WHEREAS, Masonic brethren could benefit from this interesting
historical degree, would not be distracted from their participation in their Lodges
or other Masonic Organizations and could reap the benefits of general publicity
surrounding Alexander the Great.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Constitution and
Bylaws of the Ancient and Heroic Order of Gordian Knot are hereby approved
under Section 15130B of the California Masonic Code thereby permitting any
Mason residing in this Jurisdiction to become a member of such organization.
Report of the Committee on Policy and General Purposes:
The proponents of Resolution No. 04-11 seek to allow California
Masons to belong to the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot. To
become a legal member of a Masonic affiliated organization, that organization
must be recognized by the Grand Lodge of California. The constitution and bylaws of the Ancient and Heroic Order of the Gordian Knot have been submitted
to the California Grand Lodge as required by Section 15130 of the California
Masonic Code.
The presentation of this Resolution to the Grand Lodge is consistent
with Grand Lodge Policy and General Purposes.
Resolution NOT ADOPTED.

Board Meetings
In 2004 the Board initiated a system of holding meetings of the full
Board of Trustees on alternating months with the meetings of key committees.
The full Board meetings were held on the first Wednesday in the months of
November, January, March, July, and September, alternating between Union
City and Covina. In the alternating months of February, April, and August,
meetings of the key Committees of the Board were held, including Strategic
Planning and Finance. The Admissions Committee also met on a monthly basis.
In May, the Board and Committee meetings were held in Union City in
conjunction with the Grand Master’s reception. Other Committees, Medical
Advisory Committee, Board Development Committee and Scholarship
Committee, met at least quarterly or as needed. In addition, the Board held its
annual two day strategic planning retreat in June, 2004.
Admission Committee
By Gary A. Yanes, Chairman
The Admission Review Program of the Board is a carefully developed
analysis and evaluation process that continues to be improved and refined to
meet the evolving needs of our Applicants, as well as the Homes Staff and the
Board.
The interview process and on site evaluation is done at the same time
the applicant visits one of the two facilities. A Staff report on this overnight
visit, together with the Medical Director’s comments on his/her review of the
applicant’s medical history and data, is then added to the application
information. The staff then develops an executive summary on the applicants
and presents that to the Admission Committee for review and approval. Anytime
the Staff recommends against admission the full application is given to the
Admissions Committee for detailed review and discussion during the Admissions
Committee meeting. Any recommendation against admission requires the full
Board confirmation.
Prior to the regular Admission Committee meeting, on the second
Wednesday of each month, the six Trustees, who form the Admission
Committee, each receive and carefully study the complete applications not
recommended for Masonic assistance and the executive summaries for
applications recommended for Masonic assistance. Each Trustee reviews and
evaluates the applications and accompanying data, and then discusses his
viewpoint during the Admissions Committee meeting. Home’s Staff and the
Administrators attend Admissions Committee meeting to answer committee

GRAND LODGE F.& A.M. OF CALIFORNIA

209

members’ questions and to provide additional information to the committee
members. The committee’s recommendations and comments are then presented
to the full Board for confirmation.
The number of applications handled by the Admission Committee for
couples, individuals and children continues to vary form month to month. From
July 1, 2003 to June 30, 2004, 63 residents have been admitted (one on full
assignment off assets, 57 on partial assignment of assets and five on fee for
Service). Union City currently has 254 residents (101 on full assignment of
assets, 139 on partial assignment of assets, and 14 on fee for service); Covina
currently has 73 adult residents (19 on full assignment of assets, 51 on partial
assignment of assets and three on fee for service). For this time period, we had
110 applications being completed by our brothers, widows, and mothers. During
2002 the Masonic Homes at Union City opened the Traditions unit to care for
our members with Alzheimer’s or Dementia. Currently this unit cares for 14
residents.
The Masonic Outreach Services program (formerly known as NonResident Assistance program) continues to grow and is an effective program in
assisting our eligible Brethren and their families or widows. This program
provides the necessary support, financial, housing and/or care management
assistance to applicants who are in need of this type of assistance to remain in
their present community or residence. The Masonic Outreach Service program
is current serving the need of 76 clients and 2 on outplacement. This year the
committee has reviewed and approved 37 applications for this program.
Members of the staff monitor these individuals on a regular basis to ensure that
their needs are being met.
The Covina facility provides hospice care for its terminally ill residents,
thus giving its residents a palliative care option previously only available
through the placement in a skilled nursing setting. The Board recently approved
the development of a Dementia and Alzheimer’s unit at our Union City facility
to meet the ever-changing needs of our applicants.
The Child Care Program continues to develop. All of the new housing
for this program is complete. This program is now serving the needs of 41. In
2003, the Child Care Program received re-certification from Boys and Girls
Town acknowledging the Behavior Management Model program currently
employed at the Covina Home.
I sincerely thank the other Trustee members of the Admission
Committee who labor long and hard over the applications. They are William
Boxell, Al Gates, Mark Gibson, Richard Hopper, and Ken Nagel. I also extend
my appreciation to the Executive Directors of the Covina and Union City Homes
and their staff, as well as the continued dedication of the Masonic Assistance
Staff to the applicants and Trustees.

and other strategic topics facing the organization. Many of these were addressed
